  "prompt": "\n<System_Context>\nYou are an ATD Architect. Map each Atom to its exact line_range [start, end].\n2. Identify the 'responsibility' as a deterministic skill definition.\n3. Response must strictly follow the JSON schema.\n</Instruction>\n\n<Document>\n001: # Commerce\n002: \n003: This is a fascinating \"Vertical Tycoon\" structure. Most tycoon games expand horizontally (more buildings, more land); *Upsilon* expands **upward**, both literally into the High Spheres and figuratively into the corporate hierarchy.\n004: \n005: By tying progression to the **Tiered Shielding** mechanic, you\u2019ve created a built-in \"Difficulty vs. Stability\" curve. In the Rim (T1), physics is a suggestion and monsters are a constant overhead cost. In the Core (T10), physics is perfect, but the bureaucracy is lethal.\n006: \n007: Here is an expansion on your progression strategy, broken down by the \"Corporate Lifecycle.\"\n008: \n009: ---\n010: \n011: ## \ud83d\udcc8 The Corporate Ascension Path\n012: \n013: ### \ud83c\udfd7\ufe0f Phase 1: The Scavenger-Sovereign (Tiers 1\u20132)\n014: \n015: At this stage, you aren't a CEO; you're a glorified warlord with a spreadsheet.\n016: \n017: * **The Loop:** Extract \"Raw Reality\" (materials that haven't fully hardened) and stabilize them just enough to survive the trip to T3.\n018: * **The Struggle:** Your primary \"sink\" isn't just wages\u2014it's **Shielding Subscriptions.** If you don't pay the higher-tier corps for pulse-shielding, your factory might literally turn into glass or a cluster of teeth overnight.\n019: * **Key Strategy:** **\"The Low-Tech Buffer.\"** Using mechanical tech (Iron Bastion style) is cheaper because the AI finds it harder to \"glitch\" simple gears than complex microchips.\n020: \n021: ### \ud83e\udd1d Phase 2: The Logistics Architect (Tiers 3\u20136)\n022: \n023: This is where the game shifts from \"Survival\" to \"Optimization.\" You begin to manage the **Entry/Exit Hubs.**\n024: \n025: * **The Hub Strategy:** As you mentioned, certain cities act as \"Valves.\" Controlling a Valve City allows you to tax every piece of ore moving toward the Core.\n026: * **Corporate Courtship:** You start receiving \"Requests\" from T8+ Hegemonies.\n027: * *Example:* \"The Flesh Bastion needs 10k tons of T1 Mutated Biomass. Do this, and we'll unlock the 'Grafted Labor' blueprint for your factories.\"\n028: \n029: \n030: * **Skill Templates:** Instead of hiring \"units,\" you buy \"Templates.\" You are essentially uploading \"Manager.exe\" into your employees' brains.\n031: \n032: ### \ud83c\udfad Phase 3: The Boardroom Predator (Tiers 7\u20138)\n033: \n034: The game becomes an **Intrigue-Sim.** You are no longer looking at the ground; you are looking at the ticker tape.\n035: \n036: * **The Resource War:** You start \"Bidding\" on the output of T1\u2013T3 players (or NPCs). You can essentially starve a lower-tier region of resources to drive up prices in the High Spheres.\n037: \n038: Working as a local Faction head. \n039: \n040: ### \ud83c\udfdb\ufe0f Phase 4: The Aspirant (Tiers 9\u201310)\n041: \n042: You are now part of the **High Spheres.** The world below looks like a chaotic ants' nest.\n043: \n044: * **The Core's Eye:** The \"Silent God\" ships begin to monitor you. You aren't just making money; you are proving your \"Utility\" to humanity's survival.\n045: \n046: You also work toward earning a famed Commendation.\n047: \n048: ---\n049: \n050: ## \ud83d\udd04 The \"Core Commendation\" (The Prestige Loop)\n051: \n052: When you \"Reset\" via a Commendation, you don't just get a percentage bonus. You get **\"Legacy Authority.\"** \n053: \n054: \n055: ---\n056: \n057: ## \ud83d\udca1 Food for Thought: The \"Stability\" Pressure\n058: \n059: In most tycoon games, more money = easier game. In *Upsilon*, **more money = more reality.** As a CEO moves from T1 to T10, the \"Visual Style\" of the game should change.\n060: \n061: * **T1:** Hand-drawn, gritty, flickering lights, UI looks rusted.\n062: * **T10:** Clean, holographic, mathematically perfect, UI is \"sterile.\"\n063: \n064: **The Conflict:** The \"Core\" wants stability, but the \"Wild\" (Tier 0) provides the most valuable resources. This creates a **Rubber-Band Economy.** If you make the world too stable, you run out of high-value resources. If you keep it too wild, your infrastructure collapses.\n065: \n066: > **The CEO's Dilemma:** You must purposefully allow \"controlled instability\" in your lower-tier holdings to keep the high-tier profits flowing. You are essentially a gardener who needs the \"weeds\" to make the \"soil\" rich.\n067: \n068: ---\n069: \n070: ### Summary Table: The Ascension\n071: \n072: | Phase | Focus | Main Resource | Risk |\n073: | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n074: | **Beginner (T1-2)** | Scavenging | Raw Matter | Reality Glitches / Monster Raids |\n075: | **Established (T3-6)** | Processing | Skill Templates | Corporate Taxes / Logistics Bottlenecks |\n076: | **Hegemon (T7-8)** | Manipulation | Market Shares | Faction Infighting / Hostile Takeovers |\n077: | **Endgame (T9-10)** | Cultivation | Commendations | The AI's Attention / The \"Soul\" Cost |\n078: \n079: ---\n080: \n081: I stand corrected! That secretive \"Transcendence\" makes the **Core Commendation** feel much more like a ritualistic sacrifice of status for power. The CEO doesn't just retire; they are \"recycled\" back into the system as a superior being, while the Core harvests the infrastructure they spent decades building. It\u2019s a literal corporate soul-swap.\n082: \n083: Regarding the **People Management** and **Daily Rituals**, this adds a much-needed \"human\" layer to the mechanical tycoon systems. If the Tiers represent the *where*, the Corporation Structure represents the *who* and *how*.\n084: \n085: Here is an expansion on the organizational and ritualistic side of the commerce game:\n086: \n087: ---\n088: \n089: ## \ud83d\udc65 I. The Corporate Anatomy (Management)\n090: \n091: In *Upsilon*, you aren't just hiring \"units\"; you are managing **Neural Profiles.** Since \"Skill Templates\" can be uploaded, the physical body is less important than the brain's \"Socket Capacity\" and \"Stability.\" Although, while it's not corporately usefull, the person and its social interactions is the ground on which Intrigue's game will play itself.\n092: \n093: ### 2. Departmental Synergy\n094: \n095: You must structure your hierarchy to mitigate the \"Intrigue\" risk.\n096: \n097: * **Vertical Silos:** Information stays within departments. Safer from corporate espionage, but slower to respond to Tier 0 supply shocks.\n098: * **Horizontal Mesh:** Everyone knows everything. High efficiency and fast growth, but one \"corrupted\" employee can leak your entire blueprint library to a rival T8 Hegemony.\n099: \n100: Selecting, researching departmental strategies will be an important part of the gameplay from T4 onward. Each strategy grants passives, bonusses and protections against Intrigue. \n101: \n102: ---\n103: \n104: ## \ud83d\udca1 II. Rituals & Scheduling: The \"Tempo\" of Production\n105: \n106: In a world where reality is unstable, **Routine is a weapon.** If everyone does the exact same thing at the exact same time, reality \"hardens.\" This turns the tycoon game's \"Scheduling\" into a strategic shielding mechanic.\n107: \n108: ### 1. The \"Synchronized Shift\"\n109: \n110: If you schedule 1,000 workers to perform a \"Refining Ritual\" (a specific set of movements/tasks) at the exact same ULF frequency, you create a **Stability Spike.** * **Benefit:** Production yield increases by 20%.\n111: \n112: * **Cost:** If the schedule is interrupted (e.g., a monster raid or a strike), the \"Desync\" can cause a localized reality collapse.\n113: \n114: ### 2. Memory Redaction Cycles\n115: \n116: To keep your high-tier secrets safe, you must schedule \"Wipe Days.\"\n117: \n118: * **The Choice:** Do you wipe your Lead Engineer\u2019s memory every Friday to ensure they can\u2019t sell your tech?\n119: * **The Risk:** Every wipe has a 5% chance of \"Template Degeneration,\" permanently lowering their efficiency.\n120: \n121: ---\n122: \n123: ## \ud83d\udd75\ufe0f III. The Bridge to Intrigue: \"Corporate Friction\"\n124: \n125: The management aspect creates the \"hooks\" for the social game. Your daily schedule isn't just a list of tasks; it\u2019s a **Vulnerability Map.**\n126: \n127: * **The Calendar as a Battlefield:** An \"Intrigue\" player (or an AI rival) looks for gaps in your schedule. If you have a 10-minute gap between the \"Shielding Ritual\" and the \"Work Shift,\" that is when they slip in a virus or a spy.\n128: * **The \"Discontent\" Resource:** Forcing workers into rigid, 24/7 ULF-synced schedules generates **Dissonance.** High Dissonance leads to:\n129: 1. **The Earth-Born Cults:** Workers start worshipping the \"Mutants\" for freedom.\n130: 2. **Corporate Sabotage:** \"Accidental\" fires in the Shielding Generators.\n131: \n132: \n133: \n134: ---\n135: \n136: ## \ud83c\udfdb\ufe0f IV. Summary: The Progression of Management\n137: \n138: | Tier Range | Management Focus | Ritual Complexity | The Intrigue Hook |\n139: | --- | --- | --- | --- |\n140: | **T1\u2013T3** | **Survival** | Basic \"Safety Drills\" to keep monsters out. | Low-level theft and physical sabotage. |\n141: | **T4\u2013T6** | **Optimization** | Synchronized shifts for mass production. | Corporate spies stealing \"Skill Templates.\" |\n142: | **T7\u2013T8** | **Loyalty** | \"Ideological Conditioning\" to prevent Faction defection. | Boardroom coups and hostile memory-wiping. |\n143: | **T9\u2013T10** | **Ascension** | Preparation for the \"Core Flash\" (The Secretive End). | High-level \"Deals\" to ensure your Commendation is successful. |\n144: \n145: ---\n146: \n147: ## \ud83d\udd04 The \"Flashed\" CEO: The New Beginning\n148: \n149: When a CEO is \"Flashed\" and sent back to T1, they keep **\"Ghost Memories.\"** * They remember the *rhythms* of the High Spheres.\n150: \n151: * They start Tier 1 not just with a tech upgrade, but with **\"Secret Blueprints\"** for rituals that shouldn't exist in the Rim.\n152: * **The Meta-Game:** You are now a T1 CEO who knows exactly how the T10 \"Sausage is made.\" You can build a corporation designed specifically to undermine your old rivals who are still stuck in T9.\n153: \n154: \n155: \n156: ---\n157: \n158: \n159: This creates a high-stakes \"Logistics-First\" tycoon game. The fact that the physical return of a caravan is the only way to get upgrades makes the **Return Trip** the most tense part of the idle loop. You might have the money in the bank, but your tech is stuck in a wagon surrounded by monsters.\n160: \n161: Here is the comprehensive roadmap and summary for the **Tier 1\u20133 Expansion Strategy**, incorporating your new rules on Road Ownership, HRM Experience, and the \"Fixer-Upper\" transition to Tier 2.\n162: \n163: ---\n164: \n165: ## \ud83d\udee0\ufe0f I. The Upsilon \"Rim-to-Gate\" Roadmap\n166: \n167: ### Milestone 1: The Three-City Foundations (Tier 1 Start)\n168: \n169: * **The Goal:** Reach \"Break-even\" stability.\n170: * **Management Focus:** Assigning **Rookie Workers** to the 3 cities. You must balance basic extraction (to sell for money) with basic \"Amenity\" production (to keep workers from leaving).\n171: * **The Caravan Loop:** Launching manual caravans to T2.\n172: * *Departure:* Instant cash flow to pay the Shielding Tax.\n173: * *The Return:* This is where you get **Blueprints** and **Refining Tools**. If the caravan is lost on the return leg, your tech progression halts even if your wallet is full.\n174: \n175: \n176: \n177: ### Milestone 2: Infrastructure & Road Sovereignty (Tier 1 Mid)\n178: \n179: * **The Goal:** Secure the \"Veins\" of the region.\n180: * **Management Focus:** Deciding whether to **Claim a Road**.\n181: * *Benefit:* You can set \"Road Stability\" levels. High stability reduces caravan loss and attracts other corps (passive tax income).\n182: * *Risk:* You are now the \"Police.\" You must fund a security budget. If a monster raid happens on *your* road, you pay the repair costs.\n183: \n184: \n185: * **The HRM Growth:** Your workers are now **Veterans**. A Veteran Miner produces 50% more, but their \"replacement cost\" is now astronomical. Losing a veteran caravan is a massive blow to your efficiency.\n186: \n187: ### Milestone 3: The \"Fixer-Upper\" Buyout (Transition to Tier 2)\n188: \n189: * **The Goal:** Corporate Ascension.\n190: * **The Mechanic:** You don't just \"move\" to T2; you purchase a **Failed Corporation**.\n191: * **The Challenge:** You inherit a T2 entity with:\n192: 1. **Debt:** Unpaid Shielding Subscriptions.\n193: 2. **Broken Tech:** Refineries that are at 20% efficiency.\n194: 3. **Bad Contracts:** Overdue shipments to Tier 3.\n195: \n196: \n197: * **Strategy:** You must use the \"Cushion\" of cash you saved in Tier 1 to quickly repair the T2 infrastructure before the high T2 overhead bankrupts you.\n198: \n199: ### Milestone 4: The Resource Gatekeeper (Tier 2 Stable)\n200: \n201: * **The Goal:** Control the Flow.\n202: * **Management Focus:** Buying resources from T1 CEOs.\n203: * *The Stash:* You decide to keep high-value T1 ore to fuel your own advanced T2 Factories.\n204: * *The Reputation:* You balance \"Stashing\" with \"Fulfilling\" Tier 3 Contracts. Fulfilling contracts doesn't give as much immediate profit but raises your **Corporate Standing**, which unlocks Tier 3 Buyout opportunities later.\n205: \n206: \n207: \n208: ---\n209: \n210: ## \ud83d\udcca II. Comparative Overview: T1 vs. T2 Management\n211: \n212: | Feature | Tier 1 (The Scavenger) | Tier 2 (The Gatekeeper) |\n213: | --- | --- | --- |\n214: | **Primary Income** | Raw Resource Export | Processing Fees & Road Taxes |\n215: | **Progression Item** | Tech Blueprints (Caravan Return) | \"Standing\" & Reputation (Contract Completion) |\n216: | **Main Threat** | Physical Loss (Monster Raids) | Economic Loss (Debt / Supply Volatility) |\n217: | **HRM Focus** | Experience Accumulation | Specialist Allocation |\n218: | **Road Policy** | Usage (Paying Taxes) | Ownership (Collecting Taxes / Security) |\n219: \n220: ---\n221: \n222: ## \ud83e\uddd0 III. Analysis of Potential Friction Points\n223: \n224: 1. **The \"Veteran\" Loss Trap:** In an idle game, losing a \"Level 10 Worker\" to a random 2% chance monster raid can feel punishing.\n225: * *Suggestion:* Allow for **\"Memory Backups\"** (at a high cost) in Tier 2, so if a worker dies, you can \"re-upload\" their experience into a new body, mitigating the loss.\n226: \n227: \n228: 2. **Road Maintenance:** If road ownership is a budget item, it should probably be a \"toggle\" for the player.\n229: * *Owned:* High cost, high reward (taxes), control over security.\n230: * *Unowned:* No cost, but the road \"Stability\" is random/low, leading to more caravan losses.\n231: \n232: \n233: 3. **T1 City Destruction:** You mentioned that a CEO loses when all cities are lost. This creates a \"Defensive\" idle game. The player\u2019s main \"Clicker/Upgrade\" focus will likely be on **Shielding Integrity** and **Garrison Strength**.\n234: \n235: ---\n236: \n237: ## \ud83c\udfd7\ufe0f IV. The Comprehensive Whole\n238: \n239: The *Upsilon* commerce game is a **Vertical Supply Chain Simulator**.\n240: \n241: * **Tier 1** is about **protecting the assets** (workers and tech). It is a game of survival where every successful return caravan is a victory.\n242: * **Tier 2** is about **systemic repair and optimization**. You move from being a \"Producer\" to being a \"Manager\" who has to fix a broken machine and decide who gets the resources.\n243: * **Tier 3** (implied) will be where the **Social Intrigue** begins, as you finally have enough stability to look at what the other Corporations are doing and start \"bickering\" for high-tier dominance.\n244: \n245: **Would you like to detail the \"City Slot\" mechanics for Tier 1?** For example: out of the 5-8 slots in a city, how do you balance \"Shield Generators\" vs \"Refineries\" vs \"Worker Housing\"?\n\n</Document>\n\n",
